### Research Progress of Metabolites of Intestinal Microbiota Reshaping Tumor Immune Microenvironment and Immunotherapy Response Through PD-1/PD-L1 Pathway

- Source handle: `P0507`
- Paper ID: `e41aba43ff813271768ef6cd8bbd6ece9aa8718e`
- DOI: 10.1051/bioconf/202624001009

  - **Available material depth:** abstract

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** As a review article, the approach involves a narrative synthesis of existing literature rather than a systematic meta-analysis with defined search protocols (which are not provided in the abstract). The authors organize the material by thematic mechanisms: (1) receptor-dependent signaling pathways; (2) epigenetic programming mechanisms; (3) the transformation of the TIME between immunologically cold and hot states; (4) the dual effects of metabolites on immune checkpoints; and (5) clinical interventions like FMT. The structure moves from mechanistic insights to clinical implications and future directions.
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** Provides a comprehensive conceptual framework linking gut microbiota metabolites to PD-1/PD-L1 pathway modulation, covering signaling, epigenetics, and TIME remodeling.
      - **边界与限制:** The provided material is limited to the abstract; specific details on which metabolites were reviewed, the specific tumor types analyzed, or the quality assessment of included studies are unknown.
    - **2.**
      - **Contribution:** Highlights the dual nature of metabolite effects and positions FMT as a relevant clinical strategy.
      - **边界与限制:** As a narrative review, it may lack the rigorous reproducibility of a systematic review; the abstract does not specify if a systematic search strategy was employed.
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** In the context of PD-1/PD-L1 ICI treatment across various tumors
      - **研究发现:** Gut microbiota metabolites play a crucial role in modulating immune regulation and determining treatment efficacy by influencing the tumor immune microenvironment.
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** Through specific biological mechanisms
      - **研究发现:** Metabolites affect therapy response via receptor-dependent signaling pathways and epigenetic programming mechanisms.
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** Regarding the state of the tumor microenvironment
      - **研究发现:** These mechanisms drive the transformation of the TIME between 'immunologically cold' and 'immunologically hot' states.
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** In terms of immune checkpoint modulation
      - **研究发现:** Gut microbiota-derived metabolites exhibit dual effects, capable of both promoting and inhibiting immune checkpoints.
    - **5.**
      - **适用条件:** As a therapeutic intervention
      - **研究发现:** Fecal microbiota transplantation (FMT) is identified as a strategy for reshaping the gut microbiota to potentially enhance immunotherapy.
    - **Paper kind:** review
    - **Problem or question:** The review addresses the problem of variable clinical responses to PD-1/PD-L1 ICIs by investigating the role of the gut microbiome. Specifically, it seeks to understand how microbial metabolites modulate the tumor immune microenvironment and determine treatment efficacy, aiming to provide a mechanistic basis for improving immunotherapy outcomes through microbiome-targeted strategies.
    - **Research scope:** The review focuses on the interaction between intestinal microbiota metabolites and the tumor immune microenvironment (TIME), specifically within the context of cancer treatment using PD-1/PD-L1 immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs). It covers various tumor types and examines mechanisms ranging from receptor signaling to epigenetic programming.
    - **Work summary:** This paper is a narrative review that synthesizes current research on how gut microbiota-derived metabolites influence the efficacy of PD-1/PD-L1 immunotherapy. It explores the causal chain linking these metabolites to immune regulation, detailing their impact on transforming the tumor microenvironment from 'cold' to 'hot' states. The review also discusses the dual roles of these metabolites in promoting or inhibiting immune checkpoints and evaluates fecal microbiota transplantation (FMT) as a therapeutic strategy. Finally, it outlines future challenges and prospects for enhancing combination therapies.

### Intratumoral microbiota: implications for cancer progression and treatment

- Source handle: `P0163`
- Paper ID: `487e91c7e1620d8dcd87c884be958d2ecfa543d3`
- DOI: 10.3389/fmicb.2025.1551515

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** The authors conducted a narrative literature review, synthesizing findings from recent studies (primarily cited from 2013–2025) regarding tumor-associated microbes. The structure follows a logical progression: defining the origin of intratumoral microbes, categorizing their functional mechanisms (genetic, signaling, metabolic, metastatic), assessing their role as biomarkers and immunomodulators, reviewing therapeutic interventions (engineered bacteria, FMT, antibiotics), and analyzing methodological constraints and future trends. The review integrates data from metagenomic sequencing, animal models, and clinical cohorts to build a holistic picture of the tumor-microbe interaction.
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** Provides a structured classification of intratumoral microbial origins and four distinct mechanistic categories (genetic, signaling, metabolic, metastatic).
      - **边界与限制:** As a narrative review, it aggregates existing evidence without performing new primary experiments or meta-analyses; causal relationships are often inferred from correlative clinical data or preclinical models.
    - **2.**
      - **Contribution:** Synthesizes diverse therapeutic strategies, including engineered bacteria, probiotics, and selective antibiotics, linking them to specific molecular targets.
      - **边界与限制:** Many therapeutic examples are derived from preclinical studies or early-phase trials; long-term safety and efficacy in large-scale clinical settings are not yet established.
    - **3.**
      - **Contribution:** Highlights critical methodological bottlenecks, specifically low microbial biomass and host genome contamination, which affect data interpretation.
      - **边界与限制:** Does not provide a standardized protocol for distinguishing true intratumoral signals from contamination, leaving this as an open challenge for future research.
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** Across multiple solid tumors (CRC, PDAC, ESCC, etc.)
      - **研究发现:** Intratumoral microbiota are not merely contaminants but active components of the tumor microenvironment that can originate from mucosal barriers, adjacent normal tissues, or hematogenous dissemination.
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** Mechanisms of tumorigenesis
      - **研究发现:** Microbes drive cancer progression through specific pathways: Fusobacterium nucleatum (Fn) activates Wnt/β-catenin via FadA; pks+ E. coli and Bacteroides fragilis toxin cause DNA damage and ROS generation; viruses like HPV and HBV integrate into host genomes.
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** Drug resistance
      - **研究发现:** Intratumoral bacteria modify drug efficacy: Gammaproteobacteria inactivate gemcitabine in PDAC; Fn upregulates BIRC3 and exosomes to confer resistance to 5-FU and oxaliplatin in CRC; Streptomyces inactivates doxorubicin.
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** Immunotherapy modulation
      - **研究发现:** Microbiota bidirectionally modulate immune checkpoint inhibitor (ICI) efficacy. Beneficial signatures (e.g., Akkermansia, specific Streptomyces) correlate with better ICI response, while detrimental ones (e.g., high Fn) promote immunosuppression via TIGIT binding or M2 macrophage polarization.
    - **5.**
      - **适用条件:** Therapeutic potential
      - **研究发现:** Engineered bacteria (e.g., SYNB1891, modified E. coli) show promise in targeting the TME, inducing iron death, or delivering immunostimulatory molecules, though safety concerns regarding systemic toxicity and off-target effects remain.
    - **Paper kind:** review
    - **Problem or question:** To systematically summarize the biological roles, mechanistic pathways, and clinical implications of intratumoral microbiota in cancer progression and treatment response, while identifying current research limitations and future translational opportunities.
    - **Research scope:** The review covers intratumoral microbiota across various solid tumors, including colorectal cancer (CRC), pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma (PDAC), hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC), esophageal squamous cell carcinoma (ESCC), breast cancer, lung cancer, and head and neck squamous cell carcinoma. It examines the origins, functional mechanisms, diagnostic potential, therapeutic applications, and methodological challenges associated with tumor-resident microbes.
    - **Work summary:** This article provides a comprehensive overview of the emerging field of intratumoral microbiota. It synthesizes current knowledge on how microorganisms originate within tumors via mucosal breakdown, adjacent tissue migration, or hematogenous spread. The paper details four primary mechanisms by which these microbes influence cancer biology: altering host genetic material through DNA damage and epigenetic changes, activating carcinogenic signaling pathways such as Wnt/β-catenin and NF-κB, modifying the metabolism of anticancer drugs to induce resistance, and facilitating distant metastasis. Furthermore, it evaluates the clinical utility of intratumoral microbiota as biomarkers for diagnosis and prognosis, discusses their bidirectional modulation of anti-tumor immunity, and explores novel therapeutic strategies involving engineered bacteria, probiotics, and selective antibiotic clearance. The review concludes by highlighting significant technical challenges in detection due to low biomass and host contamination, as well as future directions integrating multi-omics and ecological management theories.

#### Review planning account from b
##### Broader review uses
    - **1.**
      - **Connection to review:** Establishes the premise that microbes are integral to the TME, justifying the investigation of their role in immunotherapy.
      - **Material:** Overview of microbial presence in various solid tumors (pancreatic, colorectal, liver, breast, lung) and their origins (mucosal, adjacent tissue, hematogenous).
      - **本节作用:** Background context on the existence and diversity of intratumoral microbiota
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** Provides contrast to immunomodulatory mechanisms, showing that microbes also directly impact conventional chemotherapy, which may co-occur with or confound ICI studies.
      - **Material:** Mechanisms by which microbes alter drug metabolism (e.g., gemcitabine inactivation by CDD_L, 5-FU resistance via BIRC3/exosomes in CRC).
      - **本节作用:** Context on drug resistance mechanisms unrelated to immunity
    - **3.**
      - **Connection to review:** Highlights potential sources of bias or false positives/negatives in studies linking microbiota to ICI response, suggesting caution in interpreting correlation data.
      - **Material:** Discussion on low biomass, host genome contamination, and limitations of 16S rRNA vs. metagenomic sequencing.
      - **本节作用:** Methodological challenges in microbiome research
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** Most associations are based on observational cohort studies or preclinical models; direct causal validation in human ICI trials is limited in this text.
      - **Contribution:** Identifies specific microbial signatures associated with ICI efficacy: Akkermansia and Methylobacterium levels correlate with prognosis in HCC; Saccharopolyspora, Pseudoxanthomonas, and Streptomyces are linked to immune activation in PDAC; high Fn levels predict poor response to chemotherapy and potentially immunotherapy in ESCC and CRC.
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** Can be used to list specific bacterial taxa that serve as predictive biomarkers for ICI response or resistance in different solid tumor types.
    - **2.**
      - **Boundaries:** Some mechanisms are described in mouse models or in vitro; the extent to which these exact pathways operate in human patients receiving standard ICIs requires careful contextualization.
      - **Contribution:** Details mechanisms of microbial modulation of immunity: 1) Fn binds TIGIT to inhibit NK/T cell activity; 2) Gut microbiota remodels TME via STING/IFN-I axis to activate NK-DC interactions; 3) Lactobacillus metabolite ILA enhances CD8+ T cell function via chromatin accessibility changes; 4) Formate activates Nrf2 pathway in CD8+ T cells to boost ICI efficacy.
      - **Facet id:** F2
      - **Possible uses:** Provides concrete molecular mechanisms explaining how gut/tumor microbes influence the efficacy of ICIs, suitable for the 'mechanisms' facet.
    - **3.**
      - **Boundaries:** Includes both successful preclinical results and ongoing clinical trials; efficacy varies by tumor type and patient baseline microbiome.
      - **Contribution:** Describes therapeutic interventions targeting microbiota to enhance ICI: FMT from ICI responders overcomes PD-1 resistance in melanoma; engineered bacteria (SYNB1891) activate STING pathway; probiotics (LGG@Ga-poly) regulate microbiota-immune interactions.
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** Supports discussion on how modulating the microbiome can restore or enhance ICI sensitivity.
    - **Planning summary:** This review is highly relevant for understanding the mechanistic link between gut/tumor microbiota and immune checkpoint inhibitor (ICI) efficacy in solid tumors. It provides specific microbial candidates (both beneficial and detrimental) that modulate ICI response, details the molecular pathways involved (e.g., STING activation, TIGIT inhibition, metabolite-mediated T-cell enhancement), and outlines therapeutic strategies (FMT, engineered bacteria) to improve ICI outcomes. It serves as a foundational source for mapping microbial mechanisms to immunotherapy modulation.
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - The review covers both gut microbiota influencing tumors (via translocation/FMT) and intratumoral microbiota residing within the tumor mass. For the user's query on 'gut microbiome', the connection is often mediated by translocation or systemic immune effects, which must be distinguished from direct intratumoral colonization.
    - Many mechanistic insights are derived from preclinical models (mouse xenografts, spontaneous models) or in vitro studies; claims about human clinical efficacy should be treated as hypotheses or early-stage evidence unless explicitly supported by clinical trial data mentioned in the text.
    - The term 'intratumoral microbiota' is used broadly; some mechanisms described (e.g., viral integration by HPV/HBV) refer to oncogenic viruses rather than bacteria, which may have different implications for bacterial-focused reviews.

    - gut microbiome translocation
    - immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs)
    - tumor microenvironment (TME)
    - microbial metabolites
    - engineered bacteria therapy
    - fecal microbiota transplantation (FMT)
    - drug resistance mechanisms

### Perspective on the Role of Gut Microbiome in the Treatment of Hepatocellular Carcinoma with Immune Checkpoint Inhibitors

- Source handle: `P0216`
- Paper ID: `5b57c64d7f9c2a996379a5656507feffbf7574a2`
- DOI: 10.3390/medicina59081427

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** 采用叙述性文献检索方法，在PubMed和Medline数据库中搜索关于HCC、免疫治疗、检查点抑制剂、肠道微生物群和粪便微生物移植的相关文章。由两名作者根据PRISMA指南筛选文献，综合临床前动物模型研究、临床试验结果及现有综述，构建从分子机制到临床应用的逻辑框架。
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** 系统梳理了HCC免疫治疗中肠道微生物群的生物标志物价值，汇总了多个独立研究中与ICI响应相关的特异性菌群特征（如Akkermansia, Prevotella等）。
      - **边界与限制:** 作为视角性综述，未提供新的原始实验数据；所引用的微生物关联多为观察性研究，因果机制尚未完全阐明；FMT在HCC中的应用仍处于早期探索阶段，缺乏大规模随机对照试验证据。
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** HCC患者接受抗PD-1/PD-L1或CTLA-4抑制剂治疗
      - **研究发现:** 响应者与非响应者的基线及动态肠道微生物组成存在显著差异。响应者通常表现出更高的微生物多样性，且特定菌属（如Akkermansia muciniphila, Lactobacillus, Ruminococcus）富集；非响应者则常伴有Proteobacteria增加及Prevotella 9富集。
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** 临床前动物模型（小鼠）
      - **研究发现:** 肠道菌群通过TLR4信号通路和次级胆汁酸促进肝癌发生；抗生素清除菌群可抑制肿瘤发展，提示菌群在致癌过程中的促进作用。
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** 不同病因的HCC
      - **研究发现:** ICIs在非病毒性HCC（如NASH-HCC）中的客观缓解率（ORR）可能低于病毒性HCC，提示病因背景对微生态及治疗响应的调节作用。
    - **Paper kind:** perspective
    - **Problem or question:** 解决HCC患者对ICIs响应率低的问题，探索肠道微生物群作为预测生物标志物和增强治疗效果的潜在手段。
    - **Research scope:** 肝细胞癌（HCC）患者，特别是晚期或不可切除阶段接受免疫检查点抑制剂（ICIs）治疗的人群；涉及肠道微生物群、粪便微生物移植（FMT）、益生菌及耐药机制。
    - **Work summary:** 本文是一篇视角性综述，旨在探讨肠道微生物群在HCC免疫治疗中的作用。文章首先回顾了HCC的治疗现状，指出尽管ICIs显著改善了生存率，但仍有大量患者无响应。随后，文章梳理了ICIs的耐药机制，重点阐述了“肠-肝轴”中肠道菌群失调如何通过影响肝脏免疫监视导致治疗失败。最后，作者提出肠道微生物特征可作为预测生物标志物，并探讨了通过益生菌和FMT调节菌群以提高ICI疗效的潜力与挑战。

#### Review planning account from b
##### Broader review uses
    - **1.**
      - **Connection to review:** 确立研究动机：为何需要寻找新的生物标志物和辅助治疗手段来提高ICI在实体瘤中的响应率。
      - **Material:** HCC治疗现状：ICIs虽优于索拉非尼，但仅10-20%患者适合一线治疗，且约60%无响应。耐药机制包括抗原加工下调、CTNNB1突变、TILs减少等。
      - **本节作用:** 背景介绍与临床挑战
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** 讨论通过调节微生物群来增强ICI疗效的临床转化潜力和挑战（如剂量、安全性、时机）。
      - **Material:** 益生菌（如BIFICO）和FMT在改善术后恢复或潜在增强ICI疗效方面的初步证据。FMT在黑色素瘤中的成功先例被提及，但在HCC中尚无定论。
      - **本节作用:** 干预策略评估
    - **3.**
      - **Connection to review:** 在讨论跨体系借鉴时，说明HCC与其他实体瘤在微生态干预上的异同及不可直接外推的风险。
      - **Material:** 提到黑色素瘤中FMT的成功案例（Baruch et al.），作为跨癌种借鉴的背景。
      - **本节作用:** 比较医学视角
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** 这些发现主要基于HCC队列，不能直接外推至其他实体瘤（如黑色素瘤、肺癌）而不加限定。不同研究间的菌株鉴定可能存在技术差异。
      - **Contribution:** 提供了HCC患者中预测ICI响应的具体微生物指纹图谱。例如：响应者富集Akkermansia muciniphila, Lactobacillus spp., Ruminococcus bromii, Faecalibacterium；非响应者富集Prevotella 9, Escherichia coli, Bacteroides eggerthii。还指出了微生物多样性（Alpha diversity）和特定比例（如Firmicutes/Bacteroidetes）的预测价值。
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** 用于支持“哪些微生物特征与实体瘤免疫治疗响应相关”的论点，特别是针对HCC亚组。可用于对比不同癌症类型中微生物标志物的异同。
    - **2.**
      - **Boundaries:** 多数机制证据来自动物模型或相关性研究，人类体内的确切因果链条尚需更多验证。文中提到的机制较为概括，未深入分子细节。
      - **Contribution:** 阐述了肠道微生物影响ICI疗效的部分机制路径：1）通过“肠-肝轴”，菌群失调导致屏障功能受损，LPS等PAMPs进入肝脏引发慢性炎症，抑制免疫监视；2）次级胆汁酸（如DCA）通过mTOR激活和SASP促进肿瘤发生；3）特定菌群可能调节T细胞浸润或免疫抑制细胞（MDSCs, Tregs）的功能。
      - **Facet id:** F2
      - **Possible uses:** 用于解释微生物调节ICI疗效的生物学机制，特别是炎症介导和代谢产物介导的路径。
    - **Planning summary:** 该论文为综述提供了HCC背景下ICIs疗效与肠道微生物群关联的具体实证素材，包括关键的优势/劣势菌群列表、动态变化规律以及潜在的干预策略（FMT/益生菌）。它有助于回答微生物如何影响ICI疗效（Facet F1）及其部分机制（Facet F2），特别是针对HCC这一特定实体瘤类型。
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - 该论文聚焦于HCC，其提出的微生物标志物具有高度疾病特异性，需谨慎应用于其他实体瘤的比较分析。
    - 文中引用的微生物关联多为横断面或纵向观察性数据，存在混杂因素（如饮食、抗生素使用、肝病严重程度），因果关系未被证实。
    - FMT在HCC中的应用目前仅为理论推测或小样本探索，不应视为成熟疗法。
    - 不同研究使用的测序方法和数据分析流程可能不同，导致菌群结果的差异，比较时需考虑方法学一致性。

### Optimizing Cancer Treatment Through Gut Microbiome Modulation

- Source handle: `P0142`
- Paper ID: `3fc02630a16d49d97bf4a6310e65a5ffd483f98e`
- DOI: 10.3390/cancers17071252

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** The authors organize the literature by therapy type (Immunotherapy vs. Chemotherapy) and then by specific cancer types (GI, Lung, Liver, Breast, Melanoma). They summarize preclinical and clinical evidence, highlighting specific microbial taxa, functional pathways, and metabolites associated with treatment response or resistance. The review also catalogs ongoing clinical trials from ClinicalTrials.gov to illustrate translational efforts.
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** Provides a comprehensive categorization of microbiome-treatment interactions by cancer type and therapy modality, identifying key taxa and metabolic pathways.
      - **边界与限制:** As a review, it synthesizes findings from diverse studies without conducting new primary experiments; clinical trial data represents registered plans rather than completed results.
    - **2.**
      - **Contribution:** Highlights the dual nature of microbial metabolites (e.g., SCFAs) and the role of 'complicit microbes' in chemotherapy resistance.
      - **边界与限制:** Mechanistic details are often derived from murine models or observational human studies, limiting direct causal inference in clinical settings.
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** In GI cancers (e.g., CRC), butyrate-producing taxa enhance ICI efficacy via T-cell infiltration; however, in Fusobacterium nucleatum-colonized models, butyrate may promote tumor progression via FFAR2/Th17 axis.
      - **研究发现:** Microbial metabolites like SCFAs have context-dependent effects, acting as immunostimulants in some contexts and tumor-promoters in others.
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** Across multiple solid tumors including NSCLC, HCC, and Melanoma.
      - **研究发现:** Specific taxa such as Akkermansia muciniphila and Faecalibacterium prausnitzii are consistently associated with improved ICI responses and survival outcomes.
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** In chemotherapy contexts (e.g., CRC, Breast Cancer).
      - **研究发现:** Microbial enzymes can degrade chemotherapeutic agents (e.g., cytidine deaminase degrading gemcitabine) or reactivate toxic metabolites (e.g., beta-glucuronidase reactivating SN-38 from irinotecan), influencing efficacy and toxicity.
    - **Paper kind:** review
    - **Problem or question:** To synthesize existing evidence on how the gut microbiome impacts the efficacy and toxicity of cancer therapies (immunotherapy and chemotherapy) across various solid tumors, and to identify potential strategies for microbiome-based optimization.
    - **Research scope:** The review covers the interaction between the gut microbiome and cancer treatments, specifically focusing on five major solid tumor types: gastrointestinal (GI) cancers, lung cancer, liver cancer, breast cancer, and metastatic melanoma. It examines three therapeutic modalities: immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs), cytotoxic chemotherapy, and microbiome-based interventions (FMT, probiotics, prebiotics, postbiotics).
    - **Work summary:** This manuscript provides a structured summary of how the gut microbiome influences cancer treatment outcomes across different solid tumors. It analyzes mechanisms by which microbial taxa and metabolites modulate immune responses and drug metabolism for both immunotherapy and chemotherapy. The paper also evaluates current clinical trials and supportive interventions aimed at optimizing these therapies through microbiome modulation.

#### Review planning account from b
##### Broader review uses
    - **1.**
      - **Connection to review:** Illustrates the active translation of microbiome research into clinical practice for solid tumors.
      - **Material:** Table 1 lists numerous ongoing clinical trials testing probiotics, FMT, and engineered bacteria alongside ICIs in NSCLC, RCC, HCC, and Melanoma.
      - **本节作用:** Background on clinical trial landscape
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** Highlights that patient comorbidities and metabolic states influence the microbiome-treatment interaction.
      - **Material:** Discussion of NAFLD-associated HCC having distinct metabolic/immune characteristics and dysbiosis increasing intestinal permeability.
      - **本节作用:** Contextualizing host factors
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** Associations are largely correlational or based on preclinical models; clinical validation varies by cancer type.
      - **Contribution:** Identifies specific microbial taxa associated with improved ICI efficacy in solid tumors, including Akkermansia muciniphila, Faecalibacterium prausnitzii, Bifidobacterium longum, and Lactobacillus species across GI, lung, liver, breast, and melanoma cancers.
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** Can be used to list beneficial bacterial signatures associated with positive ICI outcomes in solid tumors.
    - **2.**
      - **Boundaries:** Some mechanisms (e.g., butyrate binding TLR5) are described in murine models; context-dependency (e.g., pro-tumorigenic effects of butyrate in certain CRC models) must be noted.
      - **Contribution:** Details mechanistic pathways: (1) SCFA production (butyrate/propionate) leading to HDAC inhibition and Th1/CTL activation; (2) Inosine production activating A2A receptors on T cells; (3) Modulation of dendritic cells and IL-12 induction; (4) Improvement of gut barrier integrity reducing systemic inflammation.
      - **Facet id:** F2
      - **Possible uses:** Provides specific biological mechanisms to explain how the microbiome enhances ICI efficacy.
    - **3.**
      - **Boundaries:** These mechanisms are primarily discussed in the context of chemotherapy, though some overlap with immunotherapy toxicity exists.
      - **Contribution:** Describes mechanisms of resistance/toxicity: Microbial enzymes degrading drugs (cytidine deaminase/gemcitabine) or reactivating toxins (beta-glucuronidase/SN-38); Dysbiosis-induced LPS translocation exacerbating toxicity; Fusobacterium nucleatum promoting chemoresistance via autophagy.
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** Can be used to discuss negative impacts of the microbiome on treatment, providing a balanced view of modulation effects.
    - **Planning summary:** This review is highly relevant for mapping the mechanisms by which the gut microbiome modulates ICI efficacy in solid tumors. It provides specific examples of taxa (Akkermansia, Faecalibacterium), metabolites (SCFAs, inosine), and mechanisms (T-cell activation, barrier integrity) across multiple cancer types. It also offers a catalog of ongoing clinical trials, serving as a resource for understanding the current state of translational research.
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - The review covers multiple cancer types; ensure distinctions are made when applying findings to specific solid tumors.
    - Many mechanistic insights are derived from murine models; caution is needed when extrapolating to human clinical outcomes.
    - The 'dual nature' of metabolites (e.g., SCFAs) means they can be beneficial or detrimental depending on the microbial context and host genetics.
##### Topic handles
    - gut microbiome
    - immune checkpoint inhibitors
    - solid tumours
    - microbial taxa
    - metabolites
    - mechanisms

## Citation and scope rules

### 规划内容
  - **Direct source:** cite the supplied source_handle and bibliographic identity; the local packet maps it to paper_id
  - **Review reported original:** keep the reporting review in source metadata; cite the resolved original study normally, with equal substantive use and no mandatory weaker wording or repeated review label
  - **范围:** preserve the actual reported findings and conditions; full-text access is not a prerequisite for using a sufficient abstract or expert review account
